### 🔨 4 Nov 2026: SWAP COLUMN SPLIT A PHASE ACROSS TWO COLUMNS (REUSE EMPTY COLUMNS ON) — LIVE at `e5e4435` (merged into `main` as `c5fd2ce`, owner's picker *"Merge + targeted run"*; pushed with the Automatic notes card from another session's push picker). Branch `swap-split-reuse` (worktree `.claude/worktrees/swap-split-reuse`, from `main` at `f5268e5`; its `node_modules` is a symlink, so unlink it before `git worktree remove`). Tracker card `swap-column-splits-with-reuse`
- **The report** (owner, before/after screenshots): Swap Column on a short phase (Prod Prep, 14 wk) beside a long one
  (Writer's Rm) moved only the weeks they share, so Writer's Rm sat in two columns. Then: *"turning off reuse empty
  collumns fixed it! But how would a user know that was what they needed to turn off?"*
- **Reproduced** in the browser pane on a copy of the owner's phase setup, kept in the scratchpad (never in the repo,
  per the confidentiality rule): Writer's Rm, Pre-Prep, Prod Prep, Production with Sim Post (offset 0), last year's
  Post, and a second Post that starts after every column is free, with a two-week all-phase hiatus.
- **Cause, in three steps:**
  1. Reuse on: `placeSegmentsReusingColumns` step 3 ("the lowest free column") puts the second Post in the column the
     FIRST Post used the year before. In the new year, that column is otherwise unused, so the year gets a 4th column.
     The frozen layout then draws two phases 2 wide in the weeks where only two run. Reuse off puts it in a column
     already in that year: 3 columns.
  2. The block swap moves Prod Prep + Production (they share a column) to the right, and the glued Sim Post must land
     at Production + 1, in the 4th column, where the second Post sits. `canSwapStint` refuses.
  3. `swapEvalDir`'s 2 Nov fallback then offers the per-week swap of the shared weeks. The arrow looks the same; only
     its tooltip ("Swap Prep with Writers (<the shared weeks>)" vs "Swap the <year> block of…") tells them apart.
- **Proof** (knob tooltips read in the pane): reuse on → per-week only; reuse on with the second Post removed → the
  block swap ("…(Production moves with it)"); reuse off → the default layout already has Writer's Rm on the left for
  the year, so "fixed it" meant no swap was needed. The partial-swap arrow is still there with reuse off.
- **Owner's answer: "Fix both".** Built (no frozen code, no save-format change):
  1. **Reuse rule 3** (`placeSegmentsReusingColumns`): every free column is scored by how many of the phase's year
     blocks it is NOT yet used in (`segYears`, frozen `computeYearBlocks`' rule: a row's Monday, UTC year; one block
     in Single Column), and the lowest score wins; a tie keeps the old answer, the lowest index. `cols[i].years` is
     filled on every placement. ⚠️ A reuse-ON calendar can lay out with one column fewer in such a year, so its
     screen and every export move. Reuse OFF is the untouched old code path. COLUMN-REUSE-PLAN.md §3 rule 5 updated.
  2. **The marked fallback** (`swapEvalDir` → `splitAfterWeekSwap` + `markPartialSwap`): only in the stint branch's
     per-week fallback, and only when the run would leave the seed or the partner in two columns of the year (cells
     outside the run at any slot but where its run lands). Then `run.partial`, the knob gets `.is-partial` (amber
     `#B9791A`), `drawSwapLayer` draws `.grid-swap-partial` (dashed amber) round exactly the cells that move, and
     the label gains "Only these weeks move, so X ends up in two columns." plus the block's refusal
     (`swapWhy`, "Can’t swap: " stripped; nothing for 'no-change'). ⛔ No chip (21 Oct ruling). A swap BACK that
     re-joins a phase is not marked (the test is where cells end up, not how many move); a hand-made per-week
     selection never is. Help text: one sentence in the column-swap paragraph.
- **Proof** (worktree `dist/`; the pre-fix build staged as `dist/pre.html`, ignored):
  - new leg `swapsplitreuse` **5/5** on new fixture `swapsplit-reuse` (made up: 2033–34, generic names, reuse on);
    on `pre.html` red in S1 (4 slots), S2 (the part-column arrow) and S3 (Writers left in slots 0 and 2), exactly the
    owner's screenshots. In `gate.sh`'s generic list after `colswappartial`.
  - `colswappartial` **8/8** on both fixtures, its labels updated: P1/P4 now expect the amber knob, the box covering
    the four shared weeks both columns, and the new sentences; P3 the swap back unmarked; P5 a hand selection
    unmarked.
  - In the browser pane (pane hidden, viewport emulated, events dispatched as the legs do): amber knob, dashed box
    on the four shared weeks, tooltip as above.
  - `npm run check` 40/40.
  - **Swap/reuse mini-gate, 300 PASS / 0 FAIL** (gate.sh's own sections, port 8798): colswap, colswapgate,
    colswapmove, colswapmid, colswapstale, the ten stint legs, onecol, colreuse (live and fixture), notescols and
    hdrfit on `colreuse`, then noswapbtns, colswappartial ×2, swapsplitreuse, colswapcache, simpostbeside,
    monthmap (colreuse).
- ✅ **Full gate (owner, *"Run the gate"*), port 8253, alone on the machine: 3,896 PASS / 3 FAIL, every baseline
  identical** (gates 2, 10 and 13 included). The three are contention flakes already on record, and each is green
  re-run alone on the same build as `gate.sh` runs it: `monthexport (month-lanecap)` X6 (the timed dialog case) 9/9,
  `phaseedge`'s 480 s deadline 23/23, `offweek (offweek)` R9 (a 10 s wait for the loaded file's name) 42/42.
  No leg outside the swap/reuse set moved, so no other calendar's layout changed under the new rule 3.
- ✅ **Post-push check on the live tree** (`e5e4435`, whose fresh build is the live `index.html` byte for byte,
  sha256 `2e61a28e…`): gate.sh's own sections for main's new legs since `f5268e5` (backuplink, backuplinkstale,
  noteviews on `wfkeys`, scenarios + scenariofacts) plus the whole swap/reuse set from the mini-gate above, run after

Owner's picker *"Push now (Recommended)"*; pushed `5f99223..383b1f8` at 22:59:46 UTC on 5 Nov, a fast-forward; the live `index.html` matched the pushed build `1e4b66cc…` byte for byte at 23:00:26. ⛔ SUPERSEDED 9 Nov 2026 by the list-first Shift box, the entry at the top: the pivot row, the mirror select and the subject line described below are gone; the one thing kept is the phase sitting directly above "starts on".) (owner, on a screenshot of All phases + To a date: *"is this confusing? when you switch from 'by weeks' to 'to a date' you then have to move your eyes back up to the phase selector box then move your eyes back down past the How far toggle and then understand that it means 'Shoot' starts on **date selected**"*; *"Mock it up first"*; *"I like it but it doesn't need to say 'the phase you picked'"*). Worktree `.claude/worktrees/sharp-stonebraker-8d2cd2`. Mockup [`mockups/shift-date-row.html`](mockups/shift-date-row.html) (today vs proposed, both live and mirrored; three ways to show the pinned phase, D2). Tracker card `shift-date-row-subject`

**As built:**
- **The rule:** the phase row above the list (`[data-shift-phase-row]`, `#tool-anchor-phase`) shows ONLY as the
  PIVOT, i.e. for Phase & before / after. Under All phases or a hand-picked set, To a date, the phase that lands is
  picked on a **subject row** directly under How far (`[data-shift-subject-row]` > `select[data-shift-subject]`, NO
  id); under Phase & before / after a **subject line** (`p.tools-subject[data-shift-subject-line]`) repeats the
  pivot's option as text ("Production — 6/29/26"). By weeks neither shows. (Before: the phase row showed for
  before / after always, and for all / custom to a date, as the landing phase, two sections above the date.)
- **One source of truth.** `#tool-anchor-phase` stays the select every reader asks (`anchorSelection`,
  `shiftPlan`, `renderShiftList`, `shiftBy`, the Go handler, the legs' `T.set`). `syncShiftForm()` copies its
  `innerHTML` and value into the mirror whenever the subject row is shown; the mirror's `change` writes its value
  back and dispatches `change` on the original, whose own handler runs (`syncAnchorDate`, `syncShiftForm`), so the
  date follows the phase and the list pins it exactly as a direct pick does. ⛔ Never read the mirror.
- **Why a mirror and not a moved element.** React renders both rows; moving the one `<select>` between them in the
  DOM would leave React's record of the tree wrong (a later removal throws), and a CSS `order` swap puts the tab
  order out of step with the eye. Two elements, static markup, the engine chooses with `hidden`.
- **`PreviewToolbar.jsx`:** the subject row and line between the How far buttons and the weeks row; the comments on
  the phase row and the date row say so. **`legacy.css`:** `.tools-subject` (30 px, `font-size-sm`, 600, matching
  the select it stands in for). **`app.js`:** `syncShiftForm()`'s row rule and the mirror; the subject's `change`
  listener beside the fold's.
- **Measured in the pane** (anchorside.sptcal): 261 px for All phases to a date (the select under How far), 471 px
  for Phase & after to a date (the pivot, the list, the line, the date row).
- **`anchorside` 30/30** (was 29): O1 both subject parts hidden on opening; W1 the rows in every state (the phase row
  gone under All phases to a date, the mirror's options = the select's, Whole schedule mirrored, the line = the
  pivot's option under Phase & before, nothing under by weeks); **J1** picks Post on the subject row: mirrored into
  `#tool-anchor-phase`, the date prefilled as a direct pick prefills it, "Post On your date" pinned, Go slides every
  start a week; I1 the mirror has no id. Plus `snaptools`, `overrides`, `calrange`, `rowheight`, `datepick`,
  `rebuildticks`, `phasearrows`, `builtindates`, `customunits` green; `npm run check` 40/40. No full gate: chrome
  only, no frozen code, no writer, no save format (the mirror has no id; S1 holds).
- **Help** ("Adjusting dates") says the phase that lands sits right above the date. CLAUDE.md, PROJECT-CONTEXT
  §7a, README, plan §7.5 and gate.sh's comment follow.

RULED: LAYOUT C; BUILT on branch `shift-tool` (worktree `.claude/worktrees/sharp-stonebraker-8d2cd2`, cut from local `main` at `77554e6`, which is `origin/main` `cd7ce25` plus docs-only commits: the plan, the mockup and this entry; `node_modules` is a real directory there) ([`ANCHOR-TO-DIRECTION-PLAN.md`](ANCHOR-TO-DIRECTION-PLAN.md), [`mockups/anchor-to-direction.html`](mockups/anchor-to-direction.html)). Tracker card `anchor-to-direction`

**As built (4 Nov 2026), from plan §2, §3, §5 step 1 and §6:**
- **Engine** (`src/legacy/app.js`): `shiftCalendar(weeks, fromIso, opts)` → `shiftCalendarDates(weeks, fromIso, opts)`,
  still inside `asShootDayMove`. `opts.part = {phases, fromWeek, toWeek}`:
  - exactly the listed phases move (`phaseMoves`);
  - everything week-keyed (`inRange`: notes, row heights, note styles, unlocked all-phase hiatuses, the calendar
    range) moves inside the week window, with null meaning open;
  - `fromIso` is ignored with `part`. With no `part`, both tests are the old ones, so every other caller is
    untouched.
  - ⚠️ First built as `opts.before = {phaseIso, weekIso}`, a start-week bound for phases and the picked phase's last
    week for notes. It became `part` when the owner ruled **"Overlaps move too"** (below), because an overlapping phase
    can't be expressed as one date bound.
  - ⭐ **`part` already IS the "just this phase" mover** that `PHASE-WEEK-ARROWS-PLAN.md` (the entry at the top) planned
    as `{onlyPhase}`: pass `{phases: [key]}` with whatever week window that plan rules. Week bounds of the phase's own
    start and last week carry its notes; null bounds on both sides carry every note; a window that matches nothing
    carries none. Its plan says whichever is built first shapes the option for both. Build the arrows on `part`, not
    a second option.
  - The result gains `stayedPhases`, for the result line.
  - `opts = opts || {}` heads the function, the same shape `hiatus-ripple` uses for its `except` / `keepView`.
    ⚠️ That branch had NOT landed on `origin/main` when this was built (its engine edit was uncommitted in its worktree).
    Whichever lands second merges the two hunks: the same first line, separate `if`s.

  weeks or to a date"*). The Shift From and Anchor To buttons and popovers are gone.

- **Asked while it gated (4 Nov 2026):**
  - *"what about adjacent phases"*. The answer: phases go by start week only. A neighbour starting the same week always
    moves with the picked phase. One running into it moves with Phase & before and stays with Phase & after. Notes
    in a week shared with a phase that stays still move with the picked phase. Offered to change it, not ruled.
  - *"do we need a this phase only…"*: no. The sidebar's start box already moves one phase. Only its notes, own
    hiatus, styles and (Production) day marks stay behind.
  - That led to **notes linked to a phase** (owner: *"we do need a way to link certain notes to certain weeks relative
    to a phase"*). Tracker card `notes-linked-to-phase` (requested); a plan-writing session was proposed as a card.
    The design: store phase + phase week and derive the week, as automatic notes do.

**The ruling and the plan (written before the build):**

- **The asks** (owner): *"Does anchor to only move phases after the selected phase or also phases before?"*, then
  *"would it make sense to have a work forward and work backwards function for anchor to"*, then *"Yes, write it up"*.
- **Answered from the code:** Anchor To moves everything. Its Go calls `shiftCalendar(delta)` with no cutoff, so it is
  Shift All aimed at a date. (The older card `anchor-to-earlier-phases`, 30 Oct 2026, asked the same question from the
  other side and was tested then.)
- **Proposed:** one choice in a wider Anchor To (480 px; owner 4 Nov 2026).
  - In A and B, row 1 reads phase, *starts on*, date.
  - Row 2 holds the choice and then Go.
  - The popover hangs from its button's right edge, as all tool popovers do.
  - The choice is **All phases** (today, the default) | **Phase & before** (new: no tool
  moves a phase and the phases before it while keeping the gaps) | **Phase & after** (Shift From's set, aimed at a
  date). The owner picked these words from four options on 4 Nov 2026 (plan §3), because they say the picked phase
  moves too. A hint line names what stays. Whole schedule hides the choice.
- ⚠️ **"Before" needs TWO bounds** (plan §2). Phases go by start (on or before the picked phase's start week). Notes,
  unlocked all-phase hiatuses and the calendar range go by the picked phase's LAST week, or a moved shoot leaves its
  own notes and its Christmas hiatus behind. Shift From never meets this, because everything after its bound moves.
- **Words:** not "work forward / backwards" (Rebuild From's words, for closing gaps) and not "earlier / later"
  (Shift From's travel directions). "Before it / after it" is what Shift From's help already says.
- **Layout C, added the same day** (owner: *"In theory, could shift from and anchor to be combined into one tool with
  multiple settings?"*, then *"Yes, add it"*). Plan §6, the mockup's third tab.
  - Shift From and Anchor To fold into Shift All's ▾, renamed **Shift**, with two choices: What moves (the three
    above) and How far (By weeks | To a date). The arrows stay. The toolbar keeps two tools: Shift keeps the gaps,
    Rebuild From closes them.
  - It opens on All phases by weeks, which is today's Shift All form.
  - **Reworded and reordered at the owner's ask** (*"reword these options and move the phase selector to right below
    it"*, on a screenshot of C). The buttons first read Everything | This and after | This and before. "All

### 🔨 4 Nov 2026: RECOVERED WORK GOES BACK INTO ITS OWN FILE — branch `backup-relink` (worktree `.claude/worktrees/backup-relink`, cut from `origin/main` at `75003f1`; its `node_modules` is a symlink, so unlink it before `git worktree remove`). Tracker card `recover-into-own-file`
**The ask** (owner, 4 Nov 2026): *"sometimes when we reopen the app, it loads the last calendar we were on but as an
unsaved, untitled duplicate."* That is `offerBackupRecovery()`'s "Recover unsaved work" at launch. Two causes, both in
`src/legacy/app.js`, both measured on the live build:
- **Recovery never re-linked the file.** It restored `b.state` and nothing else, so `savedFileHandle` stayed null: the
  file menu read Untitled and Save opened the picker, writing a second copy.
  - Fix: `writeBackup()` now also stores `fileId` (the recents entry, `activeFileId`) and `fileStamp`
    (`savedFileStamp`, the file as this page last read or wrote it), both null with no linked file.
  - On Recover, that entry is linked again: handle, `activeFileId`, legacy notice, `handleNeedsPermission` from
    `queryPermission` (a new window has no gesture, so Save's click asks).
  - ⛔ The stamp is the BACKUP's, never a fresh read, so a file saved elsewhere since the crash gets audit M-6's
    "File changed on disk" question instead of being overwritten.
  - ⛔ Everything that awaits (`recentsReady`, `queryPermission`) runs BEFORE the question, never between the answer and
    the restore. A double-clicked file waiting on `whenNoDialog()` runs the moment the question closes, and an await
    there would let it load first and then be overwritten by the recovery.
  - With no matching entry (an older build's backup, a file dropped from the list) the link is CLEARED, not left as is:
    it recovers Untitled, as before.
- **A just-saved calendar left a backup.** `scheduleBackup()` fires 3 s after an edit. A Save inside those 3 s (Cmd+S
  after typing, or an autosave tick) ran `markClean()` first, which deleted the slot, and then the timer wrote it back.
  The next launch offered a calendar already in its file. Fix: `writeBackup()` returns while `!isDirty`. Every caller
  that should write is dirty: `markDirty`, the hiatus-drag hold/release, `beforeunload`, and recovery's own write (it
  sets `isDirty` first).
- Help ("Autosave & recovering work") gained one sentence. No frozen code, no save-format change: a backup is
  IndexedDB session data, never a saved file, and an older build ignores the two new fields.

**Proof** (worktree `dist/`, port 8791):
- New legs `backuplink` **5/5** and `backuplinkstale` **3/3**, in `gate.sh`'s audit-fix list on their own line after
  `mvbarcard`. Each seeds `T.memoryIDB()` before boot with a recents entry, a fake handle and a backup naming it.
  - Red on the live engine (`git show HEAD:`, then restored and checked with `cmp`) in 7 of 8. Only L1 (the offer
    names the file) passes there. L2 read `"label": "Untitled"`; L3/S3 saw the picker called.
  - Mutation: the fixed build with only the `isDirty` guard deleted is red on L4 alone (the slot is left after a quick
    Save).
- `backupslots` 5/5 (older backups without `fileId` still recover). `npm run check` 40/40.
- Every other leg that saves, loads or reads the backup, all green: caps 5, monthnotes 8, overrides 20, halfworked 24,
  halfmarks 5, halfdays 27, mvnotes 14, launchopen 16, undokeys 11, nofsa 7, filekeys 16, showfolder 7, conflict 9,
  loadfail 6, sideboxes 78, legacyparse 7. `restore` matched its baseline (rows, cells, width, bytes, grid signature).
- Not driven in a real window: a real close/reopen needs a real file handle in IndexedDB, which only the OS picker
  makes. The legs fake the handle and the IndexedDB.

can move.

### 🔀 4 Nov 2026: NOTE-VIEWS — "SHOW IN MONTH VIEW" / "SHOW IN WATERFALL" IN BOTH NOTE EDITORS — BUILT on branch `note-other-view` (worktree `.claude/worktrees/note-other-view`, from `main` at `61542f5`; its `node_modules` is a symlink, so unlink it before `git worktree remove`). COMMITTED as `34ef08c` (owner's picker: "Commit on the branch", "Merge and push"), origin/main merged in as `775b8e5`; full gate on the branch GREEN but for the known `offweek` R14 flake, and the full gate on the merge green but for one test-only fix and three Load flakes. Tracker card `note-show-in-other-view`

Owner: *"Add a toggle in the popup when you edit a note (right below the color selector), both in the month view and the
waterfall view popups to hide/show the note in the other calendar. By default it should be set to Show from waterfall to
month view but hide by default for notes entered into the month view. You would have to enable it to show it in the
month view."* The last sentence was read as "…to show it in the WATERFALL" (it is the month notes that start hidden).

- ✅ **RULED (owner's picker, 4 Nov 2026):**
  - R1: a month note in the waterfall reads as its text on ONE line plus its day's date, "Crew party 11/6/26".
    This was the recommendation. A 2–3 line note joins with spaces. Text already ending in a date gets none added.
  - R2: edits follow it, the recommendation. Its line is in the waterfall editor's box. A change renames the month note,
    with its date taken out. A line taken out switches it off for the waterfall and leaves it in the month view: the
    editor, Delete, Cut, a paste or a drop over it. Holiday lines already behave this way.
  - R3: **"Show in month view" off hides the week's TYPED lines only**, NOT the recommendation (which was the whole
    cell). Holiday and automatic-note lines stay (`mvHolidayLineInfo` / `mvAutoLineInfo` decide).
  - R4: **typed notes only**, NOT the recommendation (holidays now, automatic notes later). The month view's Holiday
    and Auto Note editors get no switch.
- **Not ruled, decided here:** a waterfall line opened in the month view (kind `wf`, typed) shows "Show in waterfall"
  ON. Turned off, it MOVES out of the week's waterfall note into a day note on that day (`wfLineToDayNote`). That is the
  only way to be in the month view and not the waterfall. The day note keeps the week's colour (or the one picked), the
  clicked line, its place (`mvWfNotePlace`) and its month text style (copied, as `carryRenamedLine` copies them). The
  waterfall cell's colour is NOT set from a month note: the cell keeps the week's own colour, purple by default.
  Mirroring it was considered and dropped. `noteColors[week]` also tints that week's holiday and automatic lines in the
  month view, so a mirrored colour would recolour lines nobody touched.
- **How it works. No frozen code:**
  - `effectiveNoteText` now returns the week's own text (`wfOwnNoteText`, its old body) plus `wfMonthLinesFor(week)`.
    The frozen renderer, `exportExcel`, `buildWaterfallPdf`, `sheetColumnWidths` and `sheetRowCount` all call it by
    name, so the screen, workbook, PDF, widths and row count follow. With no flagged note it returns `own` unchanged.
  - ⛔ **Every writer of a week's own note reads `wfOwnNoteText`:** the waterfall editor's commit, `moveWfLine` (the
    month drag) and the month editor's `wf` line lookup. Otherwise they bake month lines into typed text, and the month
    view then draws them twice.
  - The waterfall editor's commit runs `wfMonthLineEdits` first: exact lines are kept, a same-count positional change
    is a rename, anything else is switched off. The rest takes the old path (`wfLineEdits`, `saveNoteEdit`).
    `wfClearNote` (Delete/Cut/drag source) now also runs `wfMonthLinesOff`. `wfClearOwnNote` is the editor's own-lines
    clear. `wfPutNote` switches a target's month lines off unless the pasted text IS the cell, and then writes only the
    own part. `wfNoteStep`'s signature includes `dayNotes`, so a step that only flips a month flag still banks an undo.
  - Month view: `mvWfLinesFor(schedule, d)` = `notesForWaterfallDate` (frozen) less the week's typed lines while
    `monthHidden`. Read by `mvDayNotePlan`, `mvWfKeysOn` and `monthHoldsSomething`. Both month PDFs follow, because the
    writer parses `renderMonthView`'s HTML and the print path IS it. `mvHolidayDays` keeps reading the frozen function:
    holidays are never hidden.
  - The month editor's hint lost "Month view only" / "Also updates the Waterfall + Excel": the switch says where.
- **Saved state (both PERMANENT, both written only as `true`, so every older calendar saves byte for byte):**
  - `userNotes[week].monthHidden`: carried by `saveNoteEdit` across edits, travels with a copy/paste/drag item, and
    survives a shift merge only if both merged notes had it.
  - `dayNotes[day][i].waterfall`: rides on the entry, so the month note drag moves it.
  - The sanitizer drops any other value and counts it. Older builds' sanitizers copy a note's other fields, so they
    keep both and ignore them.
  - No new top-level key and no new `fields.byId` id. The switches' checkboxes have no id.
- **Proof so far (port 8781, build of this branch):**
  - New leg `noteviews` 15/15 on `wfkeys` (in gate.sh as `noteviews:wfkeys:500`). It covers both switches' place,

### 🗂️ 4 Nov 2026: RIGHT-CLICK A LOADED FILE → "SHOW IN FOLDER…" — ✅ LIVE at `0499373` (owner's pickers: "Commit", "Push all three"; pushed `786a044..0499373` at 11:01:03, carrying two HANDOFF-only commits from other sessions, `aa5e32e` and `7899bbc`, which the owner approved by name; the live `index.html` matched the pushed build `3cb08fce…` byte for byte at 11:02:11). Tracker card `recent-reveal-in-finder`

Owner: *"add the ability to right click on a sptcal file in the dropdown menu and click reveal in Mac finder"*, then,
told why that can't be built: *"I want to see where the file is saved"*.

- **Why not Finder.** Chrome gives a page a file HANDLE, never the file's path, and never lets a page start Finder.
  What it allows is opening its file window in the folder that holds a handle's file: `showOpenFilePicker({startIn:
  handle})` (Chrome 91+; a FILE handle opens its parent folder, and no permission on the handle is needed). The folder
  bar at the top of that window shows where the file is. So the item says "Show in folder…", never "Finder".
- **Where:**
  - `Header.jsx`: the menu (Mantine `Menu`, its own `ctx` state, portalled, `.fm-ctx`).
  - `app.js`: `showRecentInFolder()` and the `sptcal-file-folder` event.
  - `.fm-ctx` joins `OVER_PANEL` and `buildSavedHtml()`'s strip list.
  - `legacy.css`: one rule.
- **Rules it carries:**
  - ⛔ The picker call stays SYNCHRONOUS from the click, with no await before it: the window needs the click's user
    activation.
  - ⛔ The right-click menu keeps its own state. Never close it from the file menu's `onChange`: a press on it is
    outside the file menu, Mantine closes the file menu on that press, and tying the two would unmount the item
    before its click. Leg case P presses first, then clicks.
  - The unsaved-work question comes AFTER a pick, unlike Load…: looking is the point.
- **Test traps found on the way (not app bugs):**
  - Headless leaves a CLOSED keepMounted Mantine dropdown `display:block`. Read a menu's open state from its
    button's `aria-expanded`.
  - The browser pane, when hidden, throttles animation frames, so Mantine's transitions stall there (`aria-expanded`
    true, the dropdown `display:none`) and look like a stuck menu. Don't diagnose menu transitions in a hidden pane.
  - The harness injects the test script into the page, so a shareable copy carries it: a markup regex for the copy
    must never be spelled out in the test's own comments.
- **Proof** (on the main checkout's build):
  - New leg `showfolder`: 7/7, added to gate.sh's self-judging list.
  - On a mutant build (no `startIn`, no strip entry), P and S go red.
  - `npm run check`: 40/40.
  - Browser pane, real right-click and click: `navigator.userActivation.isActive` is true at the picker call, and
    `startIn` is that file's own handle.
  - Related legs, all green: `sharecopy2`, `loadfail` 6/6, `launchopen` 16/16, `nofsa` 7/7, `filekeys` 16/16,
    `dlgkeys` 16/16. `restore` through the real Load path matches its stage-7 baseline (rows, cells, width, bytes,
    signature, 0 clipped) with the same saved-field keys.
- **Not done:** never seen with the real Mac window on the owner's machine (headless can't drive it). No full gate:
  the change is chrome plus one strip-list entry, with nothing in the grid, the writers or the `.sptcal` path.

### ✅ 4 Nov 2026: A HAIRLINE BETWEEN ROWS IN THE WATERFALL PDF (Gridlines: None) — FIXED (frozen edit SM1, owner: *"ok build it"*) — ✅ **LIVE at `9a18b7c`** (owner's pickers: *"Commit on the branch"*, *"Merge, re-check, then push"*; fix commit `828fb34` on branch `wf-pdf-seams`, `origin/main` at `8ed92ca` merged in as `9a18b7c` (README conflict only, both entries kept) and pushed from the branch, `8ed92ca..9a18b7c`, at 11:48:13 once the stale Pages lock had cleared (the 11:45 deploy succeeded); the live `index.html` matched the re-checked build `2d450448…` byte for byte at 11:49:10). Worktree `.claude/worktrees/wf-pdf-seams`, from `origin/main` at `786a044`; its `node_modules` is a symlink, so unlink it before `git worktree remove`. ⚠️ Local `main` was NOT moved. Gate 2's `base.pdf` RE-CUT. FULL GATE 3,871 PASS / 5 FAIL on the pre-merge build, all 5 green alone (`pilllabel` carry-rich 29/29, `retiredplaces` 8/8, `seasons` 15/15, `scenariocompat` on `releases/v1.2.0.html`); on the MERGED build re-checked, no second full gate: `npm run check` 40/40, gate 2 (identical bar the stamp), `pdfseams`, `wfnotecolors` 14/14, `notewrap` 16/16. No version cut. Tracker card `wf-pdf-row-seams`

Owner: *"there appears to be a pixel wide gap between rows when you export waterfall to pdf with grid lines turned off"*

- **There is no real gap.** `buildWaterfallPdf` paints each cell as its own `re f`, one per week, edge to edge: row r's
  bottom is row r+1's top, to the 0.001 pt `pdfNum` rounds to.
  - A viewer that anti-aliases paints the pixel on the shared edge twice, with partial coverage each time, and the
    white page leaks through. At 50/50 coverage, a quarter of the pixel is page.
  - That covers Quartz, which is what Preview uses. pdfium (Chrome's viewer) very likely does the same; not measured.
  - Under Solid, the row rule paints over the edge. Under None there is no rule.
  - Not involved: the screen, Excel and the month PDF.
- **Measured** on gate 2's own `base.pdf` (Gridlines None), rendered through CoreGraphics at 1×, 1.37× and 2×.
  29, 25 and 25 of the 60 same-colour row edges carry a line at least 8/255 lighter than the fill, worst 61/255.
- **The fix, tested on a scratch copy of that stream, not in the app.** Right after each fill, paint a strip of ITS
  colour across the edge it shares with a fill painted LATER, below it or to its right, 1 pt each side of the edge.
  The later fill covers the strip's far half, so the shared pixel is fully painted. Result: 0 visible lines at all
  three scales, two-colour edges included.
  - Rejected: merging a run into one tall rectangle. It cleared the same-colour edges but left a two-colour edge at
    32/255, and it breaks the one-rectangle-per-cell shape the Copilot import kit reads.
- ⚠️ **The strip must go in right after the UPPER fill, before that cell's text.** Painted just before the lower fill,
  it would cover the bottom 1 pt of the upper cell's text. So when the writer paints a row it needs to know the next
  row's fills: keep a per-op "insert after" list, or run a pre-pass.
- **Cost:**
  - the frozen edit in `buildWaterfallPdf`, about 40 lines;
  - a re-cut of gate 2's `base.pdf`, owner-approved;
  - a look at `notewrap` and `wfnotecolors`, which parse and count `re f`;
  - a new leg: every pair of touching body fills has its strip, and no strip reaches an unfilled cell;
  - the Copilot kit's examples regenerated, plus one line in its spec.
  - Byte-identical: the screen, the workbook and the month PDF.
- The renderer and scanner are now in the harness (below). Do not use poppler in their place: it can't reproduce
  hairline visibility (see the month-grid lines entry).

**As built:**
- **`buildWaterfallPdf`, frozen edit SM1.** Body fills (phase / Sim Post / per-phase hiatus, the all-phase band, notes)
  go through `cellFill()` instead of `page.rect()`. It records each fill's op index. Each later fill that touches an
  earlier one, the row above or the cell to its left, queues a strip (`sealStrip()`, `page.rect`'s own formatting)
  against the earlier fill's index. After the row loop, the strips are spliced in place right behind their fill.
  `SEAL_PT = 1`. Every Gridlines setting gets them.
- **Gate 2's `base.pdf` re-cut** (baseline README, "a strip across every edge two fills share"): 279 → 356 fills,
  every other operator identical and in order, `base.pdf.txt` identical. ⭐ **The clock pin must RUN, not freeze.**
  monthexport's frozen `new Date()` stalled the `base` leg (it went "pending" at once). A clock that starts at
  29 Aug 2026 noon and keeps running worked.
- **New gate check `pdfseams`**, in gate.sh right after gate 2, on `base.writer.pdf`. `pdfseams.py` plus
  `pdfseams-render.swift`, a CoreGraphics renderer compiled into the git-ignored `.pdfseams-render`.
  - S1: every pair of touching cells carries exactly its strip.
  - S2: Quartz at 1× / 1.37× / 2× shows no edge 8/255 lighter than both fills. The least-light of 5 samples along
    each edge, so a glyph can't fake or hide a line.
  - S3: with the strips taken out, a line DOES show, or S2 proves nothing.
  - Result: 83 cells, 77 shared edges, 77 strips; 29 lines show with the strips removed. It fails S1 on the old PDF.
- ⛔ **A leg that counts the waterfall PDF's `re f` must count CELLS now:** a fill followed, after its strips, by its
  own clip to the same box. `wfnotecolors` P1 did a raw count and went 149 → 146 for a No fill note (its fill plus two
  neighbours' strips). It now uses `cellFills()`. `notewrap` picks the smallest rect around each line and is
  unaffected (16/16).

  (Copilot)/`) and add one line to its `SPTCAL-PDF-SPEC.txt`: a fill that sits between a cell's fill and that cell's

### 🔧 3 Nov 2026 (22:10): A STUCK CHROME CAN NO LONGER HANG A WHOLE GATE — `printpaper.mjs` and `rtleg.mjs` get hard deadlines. COMMITTED on local `main`, NOT pushed (harness only; the deployed build is unchanged)

- **Why:** the v1.10.0 release found `simpost-beside-prod`'s full gate stuck for 3 h 20 min in
  `printpaper.mjs --state monthscale`. Every CDP call there (`Runtime.evaluate`, `Page.printToPDF`) awaits Chrome's
  reply with no timeout, so a Chrome that stops answering left it waiting for ever. `rtleg.mjs` uses the same
  plumbing. Its `--secs` budget is checked only BETWEEN calls, so it could hang the same way.
- **Fix** (pilldrag's pattern, an unref'd wall-clock timer that writes the result, kills Chrome and the server, and exits):
  - `printpaper.mjs`: `--deadline`, default 240 s (a normal run takes ~5 s). The EX case it writes is a FAIL in
    gate.sh's judge.
  - `rtleg.mjs`: `--deadline`, default `--secs` + 60 (gate.sh passes `--secs 120` and `$RTSECS`). The `{test, EX}`
    result it writes is a FAIL in every rtleg judge.
  - The other `.mjs` drivers that start Chrome (`cspproof`, `mvorder`, `hiatusdrag`, `phaseedge`, `pilldrag`,
    `wfkeysreal`) already had one. The provers start no Chrome.
- **Proof** (port 8773, on `main`'s build `494793a8…`, the live build):
  - `printpaper` normal: P0–P3 PASS in 5 s.
  - `printpaper --deadline 3`: exits at 3 s with `printpaper: hit its 3 s deadline`, and gate.sh's own judge block
    reports `FAIL printpaper (monthscale) EX` (exit 1).
  - `rtleg --leg monthexport --secs 120 --query realtime=1`: 10/10 in 21 s.
  - The same run with `--deadline 3` exits at 3 s with its EX.
  - No Chrome is left behind by either. `node --check` clean.
- ✅ **Closed (22:16, by `swap-partial`'s session):** that branch's queued full gate (port 8523, 18:24-22:09)
  finished on its own: 3,513 PASS, 6 FAIL, every one explained and none in the swap code. Detail in the swap-partial
  entry below. Its branch was already live, so it changed nothing.

### ✅ 2 Nov 2026: PRODUCTION'S CARD SHOWS ITS WEEKS AND THE SHOOT DAYS IT LOST (VERSION C, A NET SUM) — ✅ **LIVE at `ab566e0`** (pushed from the branch `b225ecf..ab566e0` at 13:58:16 on 3 Nov 2026; the live `index.html` matched the tested build `7e6b21d3…` byte for byte at 13:59:15; ⚠️ local `main` was NOT moved: the main checkout still held another session's uncommitted edits to HANDOFF, README, PROJECT-CONTEXT, app.js and gate.sh, so it fast-forwards to `ab566e0` once that session commits) — BUILT on branch `span-counts` (worktree `.claude/worktrees/span-counts`, from `main` at `ba3a745`; its `node_modules` is a symlink, so unlink it before `git worktree remove`); COMMITTED as `1350226` (owner's picker: *"Commit and merge"*, *"Push once merged"*), local `main` (`4c11cdb`) merged in as `a4f0dfb` and `origin/main` (`b225ecf`, the PDF mark) merged in after it; PUSHED FROM THE BRANCH, because the main checkout held another session's uncommitted `src/legacy/app.js` edits and a fast-forward there would have overwritten them (local `main` catches up with a fast-forward once that session commits); NOT gated in full. Mockups [`mockups/production-span-stats.html`](mockups/production-span-stats.html) (round 1, moved here from the main checkout, where it was untracked) and [`mockups/production-span-net.html`](mockups/production-span-net.html) (round 2). Tracker card `production-span-counts`

- **The asks, in order** (owner, 2 Nov 2026):
  1. *"Either in the middle of the dashed line or somewhere below, can we have three icons: one that says the number of
     production weeks, one that says the number of holidays costing a shoot day, and something that says the number of
     Off Days costing a shoot day. Mock up differnt versions"*. Five mocked (A-E); *"Lets go with D"* (one quiet line).
  2. Asked how D and the totals box's "16 off · 3 added — 102 days on the floor for 102 of 102" line share the card:
     *"D replaces the whole line"*, with a fourth count for the days added. D was built.
  3. *"I think it needs to be more clear how many total days are "off" whether that be a holiday, off day, or added day
     - we need the sum of these listed"*; asked how added goes in: *"net: lost minus added but mock up a few better
     ways we could visualize it in the design"*. Six mocked in round 2 (A total at the line's end, B a pill under it,
     C the counts as a sum, D a ledger, E a tile per day, F the total first).
  4. *"Lets go with C but I want it to dynamically hide the off days and added days part of the calculation if there
     are none. If its just holidays being factored in, we can have a cleaner view. Show both"*, and *"And if you hover
     over holidays, it should show you which ones"*. C was built, and both views were shown as screenshots.
- **What is built (C):**
  - **On the dashed line, "▦ 17 weeks"**: Production's `seg.weeks`, the meta line's "→ N wk" (no hiatus or Off weeks).
    `.prod-span-mid` wraps the line and the label; when the line is too short, `fitProdSpanWeeks()` sets
    `.weeks-under` (label `visibility:hidden`, so it can still be measured) and shows `.prod-span-weeksrow`, a row of
    its own under the dates.
  - **Under the dates, the sum**: "⚑ 2 holidays + ⊘ 2 Off days − ⊕ 1 added = 3 shoot days lost". A count of zero is
    left OUT of the markup, holidays included (one rule for all three): holidays only reads "2 holidays = 2 shoot days
    lost"; nothing reads just "No shoot days lost" (grey, no "="); a negative net reads "1 shoot day gained" (green).
    Each operator rides inside the count after it, so a wrapped row never starts with a bare sign.
  - **Hover**: holidays list each by name and date; Off days and added days list their dates; the total says how far
    the wrap moved and names any WORKED holiday ("Worked, so not lost"). Native `title=`, as the span's pills use.
  - `prodSpanParts()` (replaced D's `prodSpanStatsHtml()`) returns `{weeks, sum}`; both are in the span's signature.
    `PROD_STAT_ICONS` is `src/chrome/icons.jsx`'s family. Colours: the total in the theme's warn pair (warn-0 / warn-9),
    gained `#3F7A2A` on `#E6F2DF`, the flag `#B26A16`, the plus green.
  - **The walk** (`simulateProductionSchedule`) returns `offsHit` (a day marked Off on a weekday outside a hiatus, a
    holiday included, since `holidaysHit` leaves a holiday marked Off out), `addedDays` (a WEEKEND day forced on:
    `forced && !isWeekday`) and `holidaysWorked` (`forced && isWeekday && holiday`). `productionInfo` carries all
    three. Nothing else reads them; the walk's dates are unchanged.
  - ⛔ **"Added" is weekends only, on purpose.** D's first build counted a worked holiday as added too
    (`forced && !natural`). But `holidaysHit` already leaves a worked holiday out, so the net subtracted it twice: on
    the M1 marks it read 2 − 2 = 0 while the wrap moved one shoot day. With weekends only, holidays + Off days − added
    is EXACTLY how many shoot days the wrap moved from a plain Monday-to-Friday shoot, and `spancounts` checks that on
    every case (each wrap is Fri 10/16/26 moved by its net). Half days are not in the sum (only older files hold them).
  - **`#prod-ov-note` is HIDDEN, not removed** (`hidden` in the markup). `refreshOverrideNote()` still writes it,
    because `halfworked`, `overrides` and `mvmenu` read the walk's halves and days on the floor from it. Its "off"
    counts every Off mark in the shot range, and its "added" every 'on' in the shoot, so both can differ from the sum.
- ⚠️ **The sidebar's width follows the window**, measured: the span is 221 px at a 961 px window, 227 at 1100, 274 at
  1280, 287 at 1440, 344 at 1680, 381 at 1920. At 1440 the weeks still fit on the line and the full sum wraps "= 3
  shoot days lost" onto a second row; at 1920 it is one row.
- ⚠️ **ResizeObserver, two lessons.** (1) Observing the SPAN raised "ResizeObserver loop completed with undelivered
  notifications" as a console error (`spancounts` E0 caught it): moving the weeks to their own row changes the span's
  height, which re-fired the observer in the same frame. It observes `.prod-span-mid` now, whose box keeps its size
  either way. (2) In the harness a bare probe observer saw 0 callbacks while the leg ran (`hdrfit`'s comment says a
  short sleep is enough; not what was measured here), though Chrome evidently ran the span's observer at some point,
  since it reported that loop. So `spancounts` N1 proves the fit through a redraw at a narrow width, and the observer
  path (a live window resize moving the weeks) is NOT proved by any leg. The browser pane could not open the build
  (five preview servers already taken by other chats; a `file://` URL refused), so it is not proved in a browser.
- **Proof:** `spancounts` 8/8 on the build (in `gate.sh` after `prodends`, budget 300): C1 holidays only, the weeks on
  the line, the holidays named on hover, no id, the old line hidden; M1 marks Loaded from a file ("2 Off days − 1 added
  = 1 shoot day lost", the worked holiday named on the total, wrap Mon 10/19/26); W1 an Off week (weeks unchanged, wrap
  Tue 10/27/26); G1 three Saturdays worked ("2 holidays − 3 added = 1 shoot day gained", wrap Thu 10/15/26); Z1 Region
  None ("No shoot days lost"); E1 Ends the same; N1 a 210 px span moves the weeks to their own row; E0. Neighbours on
  the final build: prodends 14/14, overrides 20/20, halfworked 24/24, snaptools 14/14, halfdays 27/27, halfmarks 5/5,
  endsdate 17/17, snapbox 12/12, phasefold 11/11, offweek 5/5 live, and (rerun alone) mvmenu 81/81, prodspan 8/8.
  ⚠️ `offweek (offweek)` timed out twice on C's build on a Load poll ("the loaded file's name in the file menu", R9
  then R7), at load averages of 124-126 with other sessions' gates running; 42/42 on D's build, and nothing here
  touches Load (the same poll timed out in the 2 Nov full gate above, R13, green alone). Rerun it when the machine is
  quiet. `npm run check` 40/40 on C's build. Screenshots in headless Chrome at 1920 and 1440 px windows.
- **Open:** a
  full gate is not run (nothing in any writer, the save path or frozen code reads the three new lists). On the final

- The Copilot kit: *"Leave it for now"* (see below).

- **The ask** (owner): *"how could we add a virtually invisible watermark to the pdf writer so i know which calendars
  were made from sptcal"*. Offered two versions, properties only or properties plus hidden text, with the frozen edit
  named. The owner picked **"Add hidden text too"**, which this session reads as sign-off for frozen edit MK1 below.
- **What a saved PDF now carries** (both the waterfall PDF and the written month PDF):
  - properties: Creator `SPTCal`, Producer `SPTCal <APP_VERSION>`. No date.
  - on every page, `Made with SPTCal <APP_VERSION>` at (2, 2) pt, 2 pt, in text render mode 3, so it is never painted.
- **Where it lives:** `markPdf` / `pdfAppendMark` in `src/legacy/app.js`, just before `saveMonthPdf`.
  - `exportMonthPdfDirect` calls it on the writer's bytes before the Save dialog. That function is not frozen.
  - ⛔ **Frozen edit MK1** in `exportWaterfallPdfDirect`: `new Blob([markPdf(out.bytes)], …)`. That is the whole edit.
    It is synchronous (the new streams are uncompressed), so the export's flow is unchanged.
- ⛔ **WHY AN APPENDED INCREMENTAL UPDATE, not an edit to the writers.** The update adds an `/Info` object and
  redefines each page under its OWN object number with `/Contents [q, <its own stream>, Q + the line]`. So:
  - every byte the writer wrote is still the file's prefix;
  - both writers' deliberate "no /Info, no /ID" determinism is untouched;
  - gates 2 and 13 still compare the writers' bytes to their baselines, after `tests/harness/pdfmark.py` checks the
    update piece by piece and strips it. **No baseline was re-cut.**
  - The design first described to the owner put the hidden text in `buildWaterfallPdf`'s drawing (a bigger frozen
    edit). Putting it in the update made that unnecessary.
- ⚠️ **What this does to the harness, and the trap for any new leg:**
  - A saved PDF holds each page dictionary TWICE under one object number. `T.pdfPages` now counts page objects by
    NUMBER (Chrome's print PDFs and the writers' bytes count as before).
  - A leg that compares a saved PDF to a writer's bytes must take `T.pdfUnmark(u8).writer` (lib.js), as monthexport's
    X2, X5 and X6 now do (`writerOf`). In Python, use `pdfmark.py check|strip`.
  - Legs that inflate every stream skip the mark's two streams, because they are not Flate. `pdftotext` legs see
    one extra line per page at the bottom; none of today's checks count lines.
  - ⚠️ **Found by the full gate: `cmdprint` had its OWN page counter** (`/\/Type\s*\/Page[^s]/`) and read the marked
    waterfall PDF as 2 pages (P1, P2 red). It now calls `T.pdfPages`. A sweep of every leg and tool for `/Page`
    found no other counter on a SAVED file: monthcmp.py and printpaper.mjs read Chrome's print PDFs, monthemit,
    monthwriter and monthab.py read the sliced writer's bytes, monthbase.py reads gate 13's stripped copies, and
    cspproof.mjs only asks for more than 0 pages.
- ⚠️ **It fails open.** A file not in the two writers' shape is saved unmarked, with
  `console.error('The PDF was saved without its SPTCal mark: …')`, never refused.
- ⚠️ **Not marked:** the print rollbacks (`exportMonthPdf`, the waterfall's print path; Chrome writes its own
  properties) and the Excel workbook.
- ⚠️ **The Copilot import kit (`~/Downloads/SPTCal PDF Import (Copilot)/`) is now stale.** Its spec §2.1 says an
  SPTCal PDF has "ONE page, no /Info dictionary, one FlateDecode content stream". A marked file has /Info, and a naive
  count finds the page twice. Its single Flate stream is unchanged. NOT updated: the owner picked *"Leave it for
  now"* (3 Nov). Do it before the kit is next used on a PDF exported after this push.
- **Proof:**
  - `pdfmark` leg 6/6 (W0/W1 waterfall, M0/M1 month, S0 the sliced source makes exactly the built files and fails
    open, E0). Its gate block reads both files with poppler: `pdfmark.py`, `pdfinfo` reads both properties,
    `pdftotext` finds the line once on each page, and `pdftoppm` renders every page to the same pixels with and
    without the mark.
  - Mutants, each red: no `markPdf` in the waterfall export; render mode 0; Producer without its version.
  - Apple's PDFKit (what Preview uses): both properties and the line read. After a PDFKit re-save, Creator and the line
    survive and Producer becomes macOS's. A page copied into a new PDF keeps only the line.
  - `npm run check` 40/40.
  - **Full gate (2 Nov 19:56 to 3 Nov 13:23, port 8231): 3,467 PASS, 19 FAIL.** It took 17.5 hours because macOS
    services (contactsd, searchpartyd, Spotlight) held the load average at 45-132 all night.
    - **Every PDF line is green:** gate 2 (the waterfall writer's bytes identical to the baseline, and the mark
      present), gate 13 (all 22 written month PDFs byte-identical after the strip), gate 10 (the print path), the
      monthexport X2/X5/X6 prefixes, and all 18 `pdfmark` lines.
    - **Two failures were this branch's:** `cmdprint` P1 and P2 (the counter above), fixed after the gate.
    - **The other 17 were timeouts or "produced no result"** in legs that never read a saved PDF's pages:
      `stintcollide`, `loadcarry`, `conflict` C3-C6 (the known load flake; its JSON is kept in the session's
      scratchpad), `xlsxlimits`, `endsdate`, `papersize` (its P1-P3 PDF cases passed, then a 6 s wait for Save),
      `mvnotes`, `phasecolors` C7/C8, `monthwriter` tier 1 twice and the file compare that follows, `offweek` R14, and
      `scenariocompat`, which runs `releases/v1.2.0.html` and cannot see this change.
    - **Re-run alone on the quiet machine (3 Nov 13:24, load 3.8), every one green:** `cmdprint` 6/6 (the fix),
      `loadcarry` 6/6, `conflict` 9/9, `xlsxlimits` 5/5, `endsdate` 17/17, `papersize` 12/12, `mvnotes` 30/30,
      `phasecolors` 10/10, `monthwriter` 12/12 in both time zones with the 8 tier-1 files byte-identical, and
      `scenariocompat` (v1.2.0). `stintcollide` (4 lines) and `offweek` (54 lines, including its pdftotext read of

### 🚀 3 Nov 2026: THE VERSION PRINTS EXACTLY AS TYPED — COMMITTED on local `main` as `f1f09c7` (owner's picker, *"Commit this only"*), shipped in v1.10.0 on branch `release-1103` (see the v1.10.0 entry at the top) (tracker card `version-as-typed`)

- **The ask** (owner): *"version entered into the 'show info' section should just be exactly what is typed. So no
  longer with a v prefix"*. This retires decision H1's rule ("v" + the input, one leading v/V stripped). Ruling by
  picker: **"Keep v3"**, so calendars saved before today print what they printed.
- **Where it lives (`src/legacy/app.js`):**
  - `versionLabel()` returns the box trimmed. Empty stays empty, so a calendar with no version is still inert.
  - `legacyVersionLabel(raw)` is the old rule, kept only to open old files. `versionNeedsAsTyped()` is true when
    the old rule would print the box differently (`3`, `V3`, `v  3`, a bare `v`).
  - `captureSnapshot()` writes `versionAsTyped: true` only then: a ⛔ PERMANENT key, never restored as state
    (the `holidayLists` pattern). Sanitized to `true` or dropped.
  - `applyStateSnapshot()`, right after the `fields.byId` replay: a snapshot WITHOUT the key has its box rewritten to
    `legacyVersionLabel()`, so an old `3` opens as `v3`. Undo, crash backup, scenario tabs and shareable copies all
    come through here, and their snapshots carry the key whenever it is needed.
  - Chrome: `Sidebar.jsx`'s hint now reads "Shows in the header's bottom-left, exactly as typed", the placeholder is
    "e.g. v3", and the token menu says "Version, as typed".
- ⚠️ **Why the migration matters for the gate:** a dozen fixtures carry a bare `3` or `7`. Among them is `mvheader`,
  whose month header prints `{version}` in gate 10 and gate 13. Without the rewrite those baselines would move. With
  it, `mvheader`'s written month PDF is byte-identical and its print document identical (checked alone).
- **Tests:** `hdrversion` rewritten (19 checks: as-typed on screen, Excel and PDF; the key written for `3` and not
  for `v3`; an undo keeps `3`, which proves the key is honoured). `hdrverload` gains a fifth fixture,
  `tests/fixtures/hdrversion-astyped.sptcal`, minted from the leg's own share-copy snapshot. The five header legs that
  typed `3` and expected `v3` now type `v3`.
- **Not done:** the full gate. It was not run because another gate was running in the `simpost-beside-prod`
  worktree, and the Sim Post work in this checkout expects its own gate 10/13 moves. Run it before any push. The
  commit holds only this change: its hunks of `app.js`, `gate.sh`, README and this file were staged alone, and the
  Sim Post work below is still uncommitted in the working tree.
- The Copilot import kit in ~/Downloads still works unchanged: it writes a PDF's "v3" as `3` with no key, and that
  opens as `v3`.

### 🚀 3 Nov 2026: SIM POST ON EXACTLY PRODUCTION'S WEEKS AND DAYS — COMMITTED on local `main` as `140d88a` (owner's picker, *"Start now"*, the release plan), shipped in v1.10.0 on branch `release-1103` (see the v1.10.0 entry at the top) (tracker card `simpost-weeks-match-production`)

- **The ask** (owner, 2 Nov 2026): *"when production starts (or possibly ends) on a non-monday or non-friday it doesnt
  add that first week or last week of sim post. Sim post weeks (and dates) should always match production weeks (and
  days) exactly"*. Frozen edit approved by picker, 3 Nov: *"Approve the frozen edit (Recommended)"*. The option named its
  gate: every month PDF and print document byte-identical, except the calendars drawing Sim Post, where only its bars
  may move.
- **Three differences, all measured by a probe of the new leg on the build before the fix:**
  1. **A snap-off start that isn't a Monday lost Sim Post's FIRST week**, in every output. The trigger is Snap to Monday
     off, or Production held by its wrap (`productionEnds`, always `snap:false`, which is probably how the owner hit it).
     `isSimPostWeek` tested `ws < prodSeg.start`, a Monday against a Wednesday. The LAST week was never lost in the
     waterfall: `prodSeg.end` is always the Monday after the wrap's week. In the month view the bar ran on to Friday
     past a Tuesday wrap, which is the "or possibly ends".
  2. **Production's own hiatus and off weeks were still Sim Post weeks** (a Production Hiatus band beside a Sim Post
     cell). The numbering and Post's continued count counted them.
  3. **The month view drew every Sim Post week Monday to Friday**, so it covered holidays Production skips (7/3/26,
     Labor Day) and the days before a mid-week start or after a mid-week wrap.
- **Where it lives (`src/legacy/app.js`):**
  - `computeSchedule()`'s `isSimPostWeek` uses the week loop's own OVERLAP test (audit H-3). It skips
    `prodSeg.phaseHiatus` / `prodSeg.offWeeks` with the loop's own tests, and counts the offset from
    `mondayOf(prodSeg.start)`. A Friday start used to round its first row to -1, so offset 2 began a week late. A
    snapped start is a Monday, where all three are what they were. Not frozen.
  - `simPostCoversDate(schedule, date)`, beside `isHiatusDate`: Production's own bar test from `pillRunsForWeek`
    (`segCoversDate` + not an all-phase hiatus + not its own hiatus), before `productionSplitRuns` cuts it.
  - ⛔ **Frozen edit SD1** in `renderMonthView`'s Sim Post loop: `working = simPostCoversDate(schedule, d)`. It was
    `dow 1-5 && !isHiatusDate`. Which WEEKS get a bar is still `wkRec.simPost`, the offset.
  - `monthMapInput` (chrome): each Sim Post run's band goes from the first day SD1 draws in it to the last (it was the
    run's Monday to its last Friday). A run with no drawn day is left off.
- ⚠️ **Named SD1, not SP1:** branch `simpost-beside-prod` (unmerged, worktree `.claude/worktrees/simpost-beside-prod`;
  Sim Post always right of Production, `computeBlockLayout`) already calls ITS frozen edit SP1. It changes column
  placement, not which weeks or days carry Sim Post, so the code doesn't overlap. Expect conflicts only in the
  README/HANDOFF tops and `gate.sh`'s leg list, where both add a leg beside `simpostname`.
- **Proof so far:** new leg `simpostdays` 14/14 (in `gate.sh` after `simpostname`). It covers Mon/Wed/Thu/Fri starts,
  Production's own hiatus, numbering + Post wk K+1, offset 2 on a Friday start, and the month map's band. The same
  reads ran as a probe on the build before the fix: first week missing on Wed/Thu/Fri, the hiatus week extra, Mon–Fri
  bars on every row. `npm run check` 40/40.
- **Not done:** the full gate. Another session's gate (from `simpost-beside-prod`) was running at 13:26, and two at
  once stall. Expect gate 10/13 to move only on the calendars that draw Sim Post: `colswap-simpost-refuse` (Thanksgiving
  inside a Sim Post week), and possibly `mvheaderlegacy` / `mvnotes`. Re-cut only after the owner sees old | new |
  difference (`monthbase.py sheets`) and says yes, per each baseline README. Nothing committed.
### ✅ 2 Nov 2026: THE SWAP ARROW FOR PART OF A COLUMN — FIXED on branch `swap-partial` (worktree `.claude/worktrees/swap-partial`, from `main` at `270687f`; its `node_modules` is a symlink, so unlink it before `git worktree remove`). ✅ **LIVE at `0e2a4fd` in v1.10.0**: the release merged the whole branch (`3dcf88e`, the Reuse Empty Columns fix included) into `release-1103` as `200f609` (see the v1.10.0 entry). Tracker card `swap-part-of-column`

- **The report** (owner): *"for swapping a portion of a collumn (such as 3 cells only while all the others have been
  extended) the little blue arrow only pops up when you drag the cursor across to the cells in the collumn next to it.
  If you just select the cells in the one collumn, and click swap column, no blue arrow pops up."*
- **Reproduced in the pane** on `colswap-gesture`, with Prod Prep's two lone weeks widened by real double-clicks:
  - selecting only Post's four cells showed no knob, and neither did Swap Column on either phase;
  - the drag across both columns did show one;
  - Alt+← named the hidden reason: "Prod Prep 11/2/26 has a width you widened by hand…".
- **Why:** a selection that is one phase's WHOLE block (the short phase's three cells, or anything after Swap Column)
  resolves to the block swap. That swap closes over every overlapping stint in the next column (E1), so it carries the
  long phase whole and refuses on any of its hand-widened cells (D4, tested on the effect). A refused direction draws
  no knob. Second gap: the per-week verdict was judged from the anchor alone, so a selection begun on a widened cell
  (nothing beside it) got no knob even when the weeks below it could swap.
- **The fix** (gesture code only, nothing frozen):
  - `swapEvalDir`: a refused block swap falls back to the per-week candidate. A block swap that can move is still
    offered first, unchanged. When neither can move, the BLOCK's reason is returned, so refusal text is unchanged.
  - New `weekSwapEval`: the seed's run first, then the seed PHASE's other selected cells in that year block, top
    down. The first run that passes `canSwapRun` wins.
  - ⛔ Only the seed's phase is walked. Walking the partner's cells would offer the same pair from the other side:
    a second knob on the same seam (guarded by P6).
  - `swapCandKey` now carries the whole sorted selection, since the verdict depends on more than the seed and the size.
  - ⚠️ This is the reverse of the "never snap UP to the whole stint" rule (1 Sep), and it does not break it: it never
    widens a selection. It falls back to the per-week swap, which always walked from a seed rather than moving
    exactly the selected cells, and the knob's label names the weeks before the press.
- **Verified:**
  - New leg `colswappartial` (in `gate.sh`'s generic list) on the new fixture `colswap-widened`, 8/8. On the pre-fix
    build it is red in P1, P4 and P5 (no knob) and green in the P6 guard.
  - Mini-gate on the fixed build (every gate.sh section for colswap, colswapgate, colswapmove, colswapmid,
    colswapstale, the eleven stint legs, onecol, colreuse, plus noswapbtns and the new leg): 172 PASS, 0 FAIL.
  - `npm run check` 40/40.
  - In the pane with real clicks: the knob shows with only Post's cells selected, swaps the four weeks and swaps back,
    and the widened weeks never move. Swap Column on Prod Prep shows the knob.
- **Full gate** (owner: *"Yes, run the gate"*), on `dist` sha256 `f8f3d5a6…`. It was queued behind two other sessions'
  gates and ran 18:11–19:56: **3,461 PASS, 4 FAIL.** Every swap leg passed, `colswappartial` included. The four
  failures are all timeouts. Load rose from 7 at the start to about 50 by the end, as another session's gate started:
  - `papersize` and `scenarios` produced no result;
  - `mvhdroff` timed out waiting for the loaded calendar (10 s of real time), so its E0 check failed;
  - `wfkeysreal` hit its 300 s deadline after R1 and R2 had passed.
  None of them goes near the swap code.
  - **Rerun alone, 3 Nov, at load 3:** all four are green: papersize 12/12, mvhdroff 9/9, wfkeysreal 6/6 and
    scenarios 22/22. So the branch's own gate is green.
- **Merged `main` in (3 Nov, `38a0f10`).** `main` had moved about 20 commits, including the column-swap cache fix
  (`c30b639`) and Reuse Empty Columns on for new calendars (`c8fda83`).
  - The conflicts were three add/add collisions at the top of HANDOFF, README and gate.sh's comments. Each keeps
    both sides.
  - `app.js` merged cleanly. Main's cache fix is in `maybeRunColSwapGate`, the gate's verdict cache; this branch's
    change is in the knob candidates. They are separate caches.
  - Swap mini-gate on the merged build: 183 PASS, 0 FAIL, including main's `colswapcache` and the updated `colreuse`.
- ⚠️ **The merge exposed a second gap, now fixed.** With Reuse Empty Columns ticked (as every NEW calendar starts),
  there was no arrow to swap BACK after the first swap:
  - After the per-week swap, Prod Prep sits in two columns (its widened weeks in one, the swapped weeks in the
    other), so Post's block has no partner: `stintRunFor` gives 'no-partner'.
  - With reuse on, 'no-partner' asks `followRunFor` for a move into the free column. That move is refused (Prod Prep
    is in it), and `swapEvalDir` returned the refusal outright, so the per-week fallback never ran.
  - Fix: a refused move falls through to the per-week swap as well, and its reason is still the one returned when
    nothing can move. A move that CAN happen is still offered first.
  - Proof: `colswappartial` on a new fixture, `colswap-widened-reuse` (the same calendar with `colReuse: true`), was
    red in P3 and P4 on the merged build without this fix. gate.sh now runs the leg on both fixtures. In the pane,
    with real clicks: Swap Column on Post, the left arrow, the swap; then the right arrow appears and swaps back
    exactly.
- Swap mini-gate on the FIXED merged build (`dist` `d111a917…`, commit `dde8856`): **191 PASS, 0 FAIL**, the new leg
  8/8 on both fixtures. `npm run check` 40/40.
- **Owner's picker (3 Nov):** *"Merge when green"* and *"Push after the merge"*. Both were overtaken: while this
  branch's gate waited in the queue, the v1.10.0 release train merged the branch at `3dcf88e` and pushed it (LIVE at
  `0e2a4fd`; the live `index.html` = `releases/v1.10.0.html`, `494793a8…`, re-checked 22:12). This session merged and
  pushed nothing.
- **Full gate on the branch's own build** (`dist` `d111a917…`, commit `dde8856`; port 8523, 18:24-22:09, load
  average ~15-20 from other sessions): **3,513 PASS, 6 FAIL**, every swap leg green, `colswappartial` 8/8 on both
  fixtures:
  - gate 3 `sheet1.xml`: the 3 Nov date coincidence (the reference calendar's "Wrap: 11.3.26" is today's date, so
    the stamp normaliser replaced it too). With the wrap date put back, byte-identical to the baseline.
  - `monthexport (blocks)` no result, `monthexport (reference, real time)` past 120 s, `gate 13 (blocks)` no file

  - **Rerun alone, 22:11-22:16, through gate.sh's own judge blocks** (scratchpad `recheck5.sh`): **74 PASS, 0 FAIL**:

    `deleteAutoNote`. Snapshot keys PERMANENT, ABSENT while empty, sorted; sanitized (name one line ≤200, colour a

### ✅ 2 Nov 2026: SIM POST ALWAYS RIGHT OF PRODUCTION, AND SWAPPED WITH IT — ✅ **LIVE at `0e2a4fd` in v1.10.0** (merged as `0616ded` from `cb92c4a` into `release-1103` and gated there; see the v1.10.0 entry above). Built on branch `simpost-beside-prod` (from `main` at `6cc1046`; worktree and branch both removed). Tracker cards `simpost-beside-production`, `swap-production-with-simpost`

- **The asks** (owner, with two screenshots, reuse on and off): *"Sim post should always be directly to the right of
  production in either reuse column mode on or off"*; *"Its possible its only happening based on the order in which
  you add the phases. either way please inspect and find the bugs"*; then *"you should be able to use the swap column
  feature for production with sim post on, but Sim post always stays glued to the right side of production so both
  collumns are swaped together"*.
- **Diagnosis** (a scratch probe, kept out of the repo, reproduced both screenshots column for column):
  - Not the order phases are added: the same dates typed in reverse give the identical grid. It is dates alone.
  - Frozen `computeBlockLayout` puts Sim Post at Production's slot + 1, unless a phase occupies that slot in any Sim
    Post week (the "conflict"), when it went to the FAR RIGHT (`simSlot = mc - 1`).
  - With reuse on, `placeSegmentsReusingColumns` numbers Production's column 0 (ruling Q2), so whatever runs beside the
    shoot sits in slot 1 and the conflict is nearly universal. Reuse off, any phase that STARTS after Production and
    runs during Sim Post (the owner's Prod Prep) lands right of it.
- **Ruled** (owner's picker, 2 Nov 2026): *"Approve the frozen edit"*; old files' widths and headings *"Move them with
  their columns"*. The swap request retires ruling D5 for the block swap and reuse's move.
- **Built:**
  - ⛔ **Frozen edit SP1** (`computeBlockLayout`, one line): on a conflict the lane stays at Production + 1 and every
    slot from there moves right by one (`slotMap` values shifted in place). `mc` is unchanged, so the column count,
    `printArea` and page orientation are too; only the order moves. Without a conflict the line is what it was.
  - **Position-keyed stores follow** (`simLaneMovedYears` / `moveSlotKeys` / `moveSimLaneStores`, after
    `yearColumnShapes`): `colWidths`, `colWidthsAlt` (the other layout's, computed with `singleColumn` flipped),
    `headerRowLabels`, `headerRowSplits`, `textStyle.heading`. A moved year = SP1's conflict branch read off the frozen
    layout: no col mapped to the lane, cols mapped past it. Map `s<sim → s, sim≤s<P → s+1, P → sim` (P = mc − 1),
    which is also the Sim-Post-off order, so one map serves both moments:
    - Load: `applyStateSnapshot` sets `simLaneNeedsMove` when the snapshot lacks `simPostBeside`;
      `refreshAfterRestore` moves the stores before `update()` (the `headerOverridesNeedDefaults` shape).
    - The Sim Post switch: `followSimLaneSwitch()` in its listener, before `update()`, so it is the switch's undo step.
      `update()` keeps `simLaneSwitchSeen`, so a click's input + change move once and a restore re-baselines.
  - ⛔ **New PERMANENT key `simPostBeside: true`**, written ONLY with Sim Post on and a position-keyed store in use
    (`simPostBesideNeeded`), so every other calendar saves byte for byte as before. Sanitized (anything but `true`
    dropped). No `SNAPSHOT_VERSION` bump: an older build ignores it and draws the old order.
  - ⚠️ The helpers are function declarations and `var`s: `update()` and `captureSnapshot()` reach them, and both can
    run while the engine is still evaluating (the PHASE_CHAIN TDZ lesson).
  - **The glued swap:** `simLaneOf(year)` reads the lane off the frozen layout. `stintRunFor` and `followRunFor` step
    over an INSERTED lane (one no phase ever sits in) to the phase beyond; a lane shared with a phase is not stepped
    over. `canSwapStint` compares landings as places among the PHASE columns (`phaseRankOf`), and with Production in
    the swap (`run.glue`) requires the lane to land at Production + 1 instead of staying put; `mc` must still match.
    The drag ghosts draw the 2-wide unit. The per-week swap keeps D5 (moving some of Production's weeks would strand
    Sim Post), now also when the lane sits between a phase and Production, with a sentence pointing to Swap Column.
  - ⛔ **D5 for STORED per-week swaps, now by name** (`runColSwapGate`, first check of its loop): a stored per-week
    swap involving Production in a block with a Sim Post week is suppressed with reason `simpost` ("Simultaneous Post
    stays beside Production, which moves only as a whole column"). Before SP1 such a swap was refused only by
    accident (the far-right lane made it a column-count change, G2); after SP1 it would have APPLIED and stranded
    Sim Post for those weeks. Found by `colswapgate` / `colswapcache` going red on the first SP1 build.
- **Tests changed, and why:**
  - `colswapgate.js`, `colswapcache.js`: the refused (natural) order on `colswap-simpost-refuse` is now
    `post@2 production@0`, since Sim Post sits between them. Same refusal, new reason.
  - `prove-col-permutation.mjs`: SP1 widens its one documented exception. A within-week swap that moves Production
    in a Sim Post block can now RENUMBER the slot map, not only change `mc`. Counted under the exception
    (269 of 6,783 trials), and only there: every other permutation still leaves `blockSlotMaps` identical
    (0). Such a swap never reaches the screen (gesture and stored-swap gate both refuse it).
- **Proof:**
  - New leg `t/simpostbeside.js` **19/19** (L1–L6 layout and exports, S1–S6 swaps, W1–W3 old files, T1–T3 the
    switch, E0), in `gate.sh`'s AFSPEC list. Red on a build without SP1: 11 cases (L1–L4, S2–S4, S6, W1, W2, T2).
  - ✅ **Mini-gate** on build `acdb4b50…` (port 8612; gate.sh's own blocks: header through restore, every
    colswap*/stint* block, onecol, colreuse, an AFSPEC loop of simpostbeside, simpostname, colswapcache, wholenum,
    noswapbtns, simpostlabel, monthlayout (colswap-simpost-refuse), hdrrow, calrange, hidewf, autonotephases, and
    both provers): **360 PASS, 0 FAIL.** Waterfall PDF and every Excel part identical to baseline (the reference
    calendar has no Sim Post, so SP1 is inert there, as it must be); restore identical; 0 clipped.
  - `npm run check` 40/40.
  - Seen in the browser pane on the owner's calendar: reuse off and on, and a real Swap Column click on Production
    (knob: "Swap the 2026 block of Production with Pre-Prep (Writer's Rm moves with it)") giving Pre-Prep |
    Production | Sim Post | Prod Prep.
  - ⚠️ Visible side effect, by the grid's existing rule: with reuse on, Writer's Rm (sharing Production's column)
    now spreads into Sim Post's empty column in the weeks before Sim Post begins, because that column is free for
    its whole run. Nothing new decides it; a phase always spreads into an empty neighbour.
- ✅ **Gated in the v1.10.0 release gate**, not on its own branch. Its own full gate (3 Nov, port 8615) passed 2,588
  checks with gates 10 and 13 whole (every month PDF byte-identical) before it hung in `printpaper` and was stopped
  at 17:30. Its four FAILs were gate 3's 11.3.26 date coincidence and three load timeouts. The branch's last commit,
  `acffef3` (a HANDOFF note on that run), was never merged and is superseded by this entry. The branch was deleted
  on 4 Nov 2026 (owner's picker, "Delete it"); it was never on origin.
